# Proposed OEIS comments: A223914 to A230944

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A223914

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A223914>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2078](../papers/2001-2500/2078-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (41/4032)*n^8 + (41/336)*n^7 + (1277/1440)*n^6 + (953/240)*n^5 + (5363/576)*n^4 + (35/2)*n^3 + (28211/1680)*n^2 - (223/140)*n - 7 for n>1.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Aug 24 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. The count is a walk count in a finite graph, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; the conjectured form satisfies the same one, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to agree from then on, for n > 10. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A223944

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A223944>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2668](../papers/2501-3000/2668-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2) - a(n-3) + 5*a(n-4) - 8*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 - 2*x^2 - 3*x^3 - 8*x^4) / (1 - 4*x + 2*x^2 + x^3 - 5*x^4 + 8*x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 24 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A225007

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A225007>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2599](../papers/2501-3000/2599-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 16 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A225034

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A225034>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9160](../papers/9001-9500/9160-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+1)*a(n) - (2*n+3)*a(n-1) - 3*(n-2)*a(n-2) = 0 for n>1. - _Bruno Berselli_, May 02 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 1, so the recurrence holds for n > 1. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The entry posts no generating function, but its definition a(n) = [x^n] f(x)g(x)^n gives one: reading the extraction as a contour integral and summing the geometric series leaves a single integral whose only pole inside a small circle is the branch of x = t*g(x) through the origin, and the residue there is an algebraic function. The recurrence then follows from it, for n > 1. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A227081

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A227081>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9111](../papers/9001-9500/9111-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 3*n*a(n) +2*(-13*n+9)*a(n-1) +4*(13*n-21)*a(n-2) +24*(-n+2)*a(n-3)=0.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 1, so the recurrence holds for n > 1. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A227161

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A227161>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [123](../papers/0001-0500/0123-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/24)*n^4 + (1/12)*n^3 + (23/24)*n^2 + (11/12)*n + 1.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured closed form is correct. The entry states a generating function as fact; a linear recurrence with polynomial coefficients follows from it exactly, the conjectured form satisfies the same recurrence, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to be equal from then on. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228278

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228278>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2817](../papers/2501-3000/2817-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228329

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228329>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [8612](../papers/8501-9000/8612-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(2*n+1)*a(n) + 2*(-26*n^2+25*n-11)*a(n-1) + 20*(4*n-5)*(4*n-7)*a(n-2) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Sep 08 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228330

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228330>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [9295](../papers/9001-9500/9295-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(2*n+1)*(3467*n-4029)*a(n) + 8*(-36721*n^3 + 109040*n^2 - 137926*n + 69822)*a(n-1) + 4*(4*n-9)*(45706*n-7907)*(4*n-7)*a(n-2) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Sep 08 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228385

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228385>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8231](../papers/8001-8500/8231-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + 6*a(n-2) - a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 - x)*(1 + x) / (1 - 2*x - 6*x^2 + x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 11 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 8 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228960

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228960>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [9290](../papers/9001-9500/9290-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 3*n*(1661*n-1820) *(3*n-1) *(3*n-2) *a(n) +(1927258*n^4 -17091925*n^3 +50171975*n^2 -59448794*n +24442440) *a(n-1) +12*(-705605*n^4 +6363374*n^3 -20228575*n^2 +27219817*n -13185456) *a(n-2) +54*(n-2) *(250934*n^3 -1892927*n^2 +4367836*n -3055963) *a(n-3) -486*(n-2)*(n-3) *(26090*n-34343) *(2*n-7) *a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Sep 15 2013.
> Conjecture 1: the supercongruence a(n*p^r) == a(n*p^(r-1)) (mod p^(2*r)) holds for positive integers n and r and all primes p >= 5. Cf. A350383.
> Conjecture 2: let k be a positive integer, m an integer and let f(x) = g(x)/h(x), where both g(x) and h(x) are finite products of cyclotomic polynomials. Then the same supercongruences hold, except for a finite number of primes p depending on f(x), for the sequence {a_(k,m,f)(n): n >= 0} defined by a_(k,m,f)(n) = [x^(k*n)] f(x)^(m*n). (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A229397

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A229397>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [8242](../papers/8001-8500/8242-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) - 6*a(n-2) + 3*a(n-3) - a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 - 7*x + 3*x^2 - x^3) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 4*x + 2*x^2 - x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 14 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 5 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 17 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 16. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026

## A230647

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230647>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [8243](../papers/8001-8500/8243-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*a(n-2) -4*a(n-4).
> Empirical: G.f. -3*x^2*(2*x+1)*(x-1) / ( 1-9*x^2+4*x^4 ). - _R. J. Mathar_, Oct 27 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Only the cells with i+j even carry a value, and the diagonal and antidiagonal neighbours of such a cell are again such cells, so the white squares form a board of their own on which the condition looks one line up and one line down. Three consecutive lines therefore settle it for the middle one, and counting a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 5 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(1 + 8*x + x^2 - 12*x^3 - 14*x^4 + 4*x^5 + 8*x^6) / (1 - 16*x^2 - 3*x^4 + 10*x^6 - 24*x^8 + 16*x^10). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 23 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 473 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A230942

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230942>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [7954](../papers/7501-8000/7954-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 14*a(n-3) + a(n-4) - a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(3 - x + 14*x^2 + x^3 - x^4) / (1 - 3*x - 14*x^3 - x^4 + x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 18 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Only the cells with i+j even carry a value, and the diagonal and antidiagonal neighbours of such a cell are again such cells, so the white squares form a board of their own on which the condition looks one line up and one line down. Three consecutive lines therefore settle it for the middle one, and counting a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 9 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
